CSC
Hon'ble Rajesh Singh Chauhan,J.
1. Heard Sri Pradeep Agarwal, learned counsel for the petitioners, Sri
Kuldeep Pati Tripathi, learned Additional Advocate General assisted by
Sri Alok Gupta, Advocate and Sri Vivek Shukla, learned Addl. C.S.C. for
the State respondents.
2. Sri Agarwal has filed supplementary rejoinder affidavit, same is
taken on record.
3. In this bunch of writ petitions mainly the Government Orders dated
23.7.1987 read with clarificatory Government Order dated 6.12.1990,
clause IX of the Government Order dated 23.12.2016 and the
Government Order dated 08.12.2008 have been assailed and the
petitioners have prayed for restoration of commuted pension after expiry
of 10 years 11 months or 11 years instead of 15 years ignoring the
impugned directions issued in the impugned government orders.

184.
4. This is the bunch of writ petitions having similar and identical
question of law and facts, therefore, with the consent of learned counsel
for the rival parties, all the writ petitions connected together and are
being decided by a common judgment.
5. Mainly the grievance of all the petitioners in the bunch of writ
petitions is that the concerning opposite parties are deducting the amont
from the monthly pension for the period of 180 months (15 years)
whereas the commutation amount paid at the time of retirement and the
interest thereupon is fully recovered within a period of 131 months (10
years and 11 months) or 11 years at the most.
6. As per learned counsel for the petitioners when the amount
deducted from the monthly pension in lieu of lump sum amount
commuted at the time of retirement, multiplied with the restoration period
i.e. 180 months (15 years) as stipulated in the U.P. Civil Pension
(Commutation) Rules, 1941 and G.O. No. 3-1508/10-2008-308-97 dated
8.12.2008 issued by the Finance General, Section -3 of the State of U.P.,
then the value of recovered amount and interest thereon is much more
than the commutation amount.
7. Learned counsel for the petitioner has stated that 15 years of
restoration of commuted portion of pension had been fixed by the State
when the life expectancy in India was comparatively much lower. The life
expectancy was 57 years only whereas the retirement age was little bit
higher than that. However, at present the average life expectancy in the
country is about 70.42 years which is much more than the age of
retirement on superannuation. The life expectancy of government
employee is even higher than the life expectancy of population in
general. Thus, on account of increased life expectancy and reduced
mortality rate, 15 years of period of restoration is not sustainable and as
such must be reduced from 15 years to 10 years 11 months or at the
most 11 years.
8. Learned counsel for the petitioners have submitted that 8% rate of
interest per annum w.e.f. 1.1.2006 for fixation of commutation factor
(C.F.) is very high as compared to gratuity and Provident Fund (GPF)

185.
rate of interest is 7.1 % after 2020 and other government rates on
deposits. The Reserve Bank of India repo rate was 6.5% as on
December, 2008 and stared declining from 2019 on-wards to 4% and
once again in 2023 as it was 6.5%. The mean rate of interest on the
fixed deposits in the State Bank of India varies from 7% per annum to
7.5 % per annum.
9. The restoration of full pension after 15 years instead of 10 years
and 11 months is resulting in unjust enrichment of the State at the
expense / loss of senior citizens and it is contrary to the duty and
responsibility of a welfare State to act a a 'Model Employer'.
10. Learned counsel for the petitioners have drawn attention of this
Court towards R.A.-2 of rejoinder affidavit dated 17.3.2025 which is a
photocopy of relevant chapter of the Vth Central Pay Commission along
with commutation of pension to show that considering the aspect of
commutation of pension thoroughly in the light of the judgment of
constitutional courts the pay commission recommended that it would be
reasonable to restore the commuted portion of pension after 12 years
instead of 15 years as at present.
11. Learned counsel for the petitioners have also referred Annexure
R.A.-3 which is the photocopy of Chapter III regarding commutation of
pension of the Second National Judicial Pay Commission having
committee of three eminent persons namely Justice P.V. Reddy, Former
Judge, Supreme Court of India (Chairman), Justice R. Basant, Former
Judge, High Court of Kerla (Member) and Sri Vinay Kumar Gupta,
District Judge, Delhi (Member Secretary) showing conclusion thereof
which reads as under :
"i. Restoration period of 12 years suggested by V CPC appears to
be more than adequate. Infact as per the workings given by the
Commission supra, it is seen that the lump sum paid in lieu of
commutation stands recovered with interest rate of 8% p.a. within 11
years. Even after giving due allowance to unforeseen contingencies,
prima facle, it is reasonable to conclude that the restoration ought to
take place on the expiry of 12 years and not beyond that.

186.
ii. Prima facie it appears that the judgment of Hon'ble Supreme
Court rendered in the year 1986 [(1987) 1 SCC 142] has lost its
relevance in the present day context and cannot be taken as a
binding precedent for all time to come. There are certain general
observations which were primarily meant to give a quietus to the
issue of commutation at that point of time, keeping in view the offer
made by the Central Government for the first time to confer the
benefit of commutation and restoration of full pension after a certain
period.
iii. The issue regarding the restoration period needs to be examined
denovo after issue of notice to Government of India (Department of
Pension and Pensioners' Welfare) and on consideration of the
material placed before the Hon'ble Court."
12. Therefore, Sri Agarwal has submitted that even if the
recommendation of Vth Central Pay Commission as well as of IInd
National Judicial Pay Commission is considered on the positive notes,
the commuted portion of pension should be restored after a period of 12
years reckoning from the actual date of commutation.
13. Per contra, learned counsel for the opposite parties have
submitted that at the time of receiving the benefit of commutation of
pension the employees were very much aware that the original penson
would be restored after completion of 15 years, therefore, a binding
contract comes into existence between the employee and the employer
to that effect. Furtehr , having acquiesced to the commutation policy with
open eyes, it is not open for retiring employee to contend a later that the
period of restoration of full pension be reduced from 15 years to 10 years
and 11 months or 11 years or 12 years.
14. Learned counsel for the opposite parties have stated that the
aforesaid fact has been thoroughly considered by the Division Bench of
this Court vide judgment and order dated 15.1.2025 in re: Writ-A No.
17819 of 2024 : Ashok Kumar Agarwal and 48 others vs. Union of India
and others. For the convenience the order dated 15.1.2025 is being
reproduced herein below :

187.
"Petitioners are retired employees of Punjab National Bank. At the time of
their superannuation, they availed of the benefit of commutation of part of
their pension in terms of the applicable Service Regulations i.e. Punjab
National Bank (Employee) Pension Regulation, 1995 (hereinafter referred to
as the Regulations of 1995). The Pension Regulations clearly contemplated
that in the event an employee avails benefit of commutation of pension, he
would be entitled to lumpsum amount on the commutation of his giving up
pension, upto 1/3rd of the pension. The Regulations of 1995 clearly
contemplate that pension would be restored after a period of 15 years.
The petitioners contend that actual amount of deduction on account of 1/3rd
reduction in the pension, due to commutation for a period of 10-11 years,
would almost equalise the lumpsum amount paid on commutation, and
therefore, the period of 15 years fixed for restoration of pension be reduced
to 10 years. For such purposes, the petitioners lay challenge to Regulation
41(4) and 41(5) of the Regulations of 1995, which are reproduced
hereinafter:
"(4) In the case of a pensioner eligible for superannuation pension or
pension on voluntary retirement or premature retirement pension, no
medical examination shall be necessary, if the application for commutation
is made within one year from the date of retirement. However, if such a
pensioner applies for commutation of pension after one year from the date
of his retirement, the same will be permitted subject to medical examination;
Provided that in the case of an applicant who is in receipt of a provisional
pension as in Regulation 46 and for whom pension in whole or in part on the
finalisation of the departmental or judicial proceedings has been authorised,
the period of one year referred to in this sub-regulation shall reckon from the
date of issue of the orders consequent upon the finalisation of the
departmental or judicial proceedings.
(5) An applicant who -
(i) retires on invalid pension under regulation 30 of these regulations; or
(ii) is in receipt of compassionate allowance under regulation 31 of these
regulations; or
(iii) is compulsory retired by the Bank and is eligible for compulsory
retirement pension under regulation 33 shall be eligible to commute a
fraction of his pension subject to the limit specified in sub-regulation (1) after
he has been declared fit by a medical officer approved by the bank."

188.
In the writ petition, the petitioners have furnished detail in respect of
lumpsum amount paid to the petitioners, consequent upon commutation of
pension, as also the actual deduction made on account of 1/3rd reduction in
their pension to submit that in fact the benefit extended by the bank, on
account of commutation, gets equalised on the expiry of 10-11 years itself
and therefore, the Service Regulations providing for restoration of pension
upon expiry of 15 years, ought to be interfered with, by this Court and
reduced to 10 years.
This petition is opposed by learned counsel for the respondent-Bank, who
states that statutory Regulations clearly extend an offer/option to the retiring
bank employee to avail receipt of lumpsum amount in lieu of giving up
specified percent of pension on account of its commutation. Argument is
that once the petitioners accept such offer/option and avail the benefit of
receipt of lumpsum amount, it would not be open for the petitioners to
question the term after which alone the pension gets restored.
We have heard Sri Pradeep Verma, learned counsel for the petitioners and
Sri Ashok Bhatnagar, learned counsel for the respondent-Bank, and have
perused the materials on record.
Clause 41 (1) to (3) of the statutory Regulations of 1995 are reproduced
hereinafter:
"(1) An employee shall be entitled to commute for a lump sum payment of a
fraction not exceeding one-third of his pension:
Provided that in respect of an employee who is governed by sub-regulation
(5) of Regulation 3 of these regulations, the family of such employee shall
also be entitled to commute for a lump sum payment a fraction not
exceeding one- third of the pension admissible to the employee.
(2) An employee shall indicate the fraction of pension, which he desires to
commute, and may either indicate the maximum limit of one-third pension or
such lower limit, as he may desire to commute.
(3) if fraction of pension to be commuted results in fraction of rupee, such
fraction of a rupee shall be ignored for the purpose of commutation."
Clause 41(4) contains reference to a table, which indicates the commutation
amount as also the manner of its calculation. The table is followed with
certain notes, which also highlight the manner in which the computation is to
be carried out for the purposes of commutation. Clause 2 of the note is
relevant and is reproduced hereinafter:

189.
"(2) An employee who had commuted the admissible portion of pension is
entitled to have the commuted portion of the pension restored after the
expiry of a period of fifteen years from the date of commutation."
The provisions contained in the Reggivings about it, they could have sought
appropriate clarification before accepting the offer. Once, the petitioners
have acquiesced to the policy and accepted the offer, their subsequent
attempt to resile or seek change in its computation would clearly be
impermissible.
The writ petition lacks merit and is, accordingly, dismissed.
The view taken by us clearly finds support from the adjudication made by
the Supreme Court in "Common Cause", A Registered Society And Others
Vs. Union of India, (1987) 1 Supreme Court Cases, 142, R. Gandhi Vs.
Union of India And Others, (1999) 8 SCC 106 as well the judgment of Delhi
High Court in Forum Retired IPS Officers (FORIPSO) Vs. Union of India &
Another, 2019 SCC Online Del 6610 and Punjab & Haryana High Court in
Shila Devi Vs. State of Punjab in CWP No. 9426 of 2023."ulations would
clearly indicate that the retiring employee shall indicate a fraction of
pension, which he desires to commute, and may indicate its maximum limit,
which shall not be more than 1/3rd of the pension. In case, the fraction
amount is in part of the rupee, such fraction of a rupee is to be ignored for
the purposes of commutation. Note (2) is categorical and provides in
specific terms that employees who have commuted the admissible portion
of pension, is entitled to have the commuted portion of the pension restored
after the expiry of 15 years from the date of commutation. The statutory
scheme is, therefore, abundantly clear that an option is extended to the
retiring employee concern to avail of the benefit of computation and such
computation is on specific terms that on expiry of 15 years of such
commutation, the original pension is to be restored.
The petitioners' contention that period for resumption of full pension be
reduced from 15 years to 10 years only because the bank actually recovers
the lumpsum amount paid on expiry of 10 years, is a misconceived
argument. The Policy contained in the Regulations of 1995 extends an offer
to the retiring employee to avail the benefit of commutation on specific
terms. These terms clearly provide for restoration of pension only on expiry
of 15 years. The petitioners otherwise do not say that the terms of the policy
is unconstitutional or unconscionable.
Having accepted such offer, a binding contract comes into existence
between the employee and the employer as per which the original pension

190.
is to be restored after 15 years. Having acquiesced to the commutation
policy with open eyes, it is not open for the retiring employee to contend
later that the period of restoration of full pension be reduced from 15 years
to 10 years. Whether or not the lumpsum amount gets equalised on expiry
of 10 years or 11 year is not decisive or material. What is material is the
nature of obligation which enures upon the parties when the retiring
employee accepts the provision of commutation of pension. The employee
with his open eyes having availed the policy, cannot subsequently turn
around or seek modification in its terms. The argument that the table or the
figures were not adequately disclosed, is also not acceptable, inasmuch as
the chart specifies the manner in which the commutation is to be fixed and
the period after which the original pension is to be restored. In case, the
employees had any misgivings about it, they could have sought appropriate
clarification before accepting the offer. Once, the petitioners have
acquiesced to the policy and accepted the offer, their subsequent attempt to
resile or seek change in its computation would clearly be impermissible.
The writ petition lacks merit and is, accordingly, dismissed.
The view taken by us clearly finds support from the adjudication made by
the Supreme Court in "Common Cause", A Registered Society And Others
Vs. Union of India, (1987) 1 Supreme Court Cases, 142, R. Gandhi Vs.
Union of India And Others, (1999) 8 SCC 106 as well the judgment of Delhi
High Court in Forum Retired IPS Officers (FORIPSO) Vs. Union of India &
Another, 2019 SCC Online Del 6610 and Punjab & Haryana High Court in
Shila Devi Vs. State of Punjab in CWP No. 9426 of 2023."
15. Counsel for the opposite parties have also stated that considering
the aforesaid judgment of this Court the single judge in Writ-A No. 20906
of 2024 : Sobaran Singh and others vs. State of U.P. & others dismissed
the similar writ petition vide judgment and order dated 13.2.2025.
16. Learned counsel for the opposite parties have also drawn attention
of this Court towards Annexure S.A.-1 of the supplementary affidavit
dated 10.1.2025 which is a copy of the judgment and order dated
27.11.2024 passed by the High Court of Punjab and Haryana at
Chandigarh in bunch of writ petitions having leading Writ No. 9426 of
2023 (O&M) Sheela Devi and others vs. State of Punjab and others.
Para nos. 27 to 29 of the aforesaid judgment reads as under :

191.
"27. It is a matter of record that all the petitioners before us are retired
employees who have admittedly availed of the benefit of commutation
of pension. Admittedly, pension of some of the employees also stands
restored. All the petitioners were in service at the time of issuance of
notification dated 21.07.1998. They never raised any objection to the
stipulated period of 15 years for restoration of pension. Having availed
of a benefit which is clearly voluntary in nature, it is not open to the
petitioners to raise the grievances as noted above, at this stage, to
seek a variation in the terms and conditions accepted by them with
open eyes. They are not entitled to seek recovery of the amount so
deposited by them in accordance with the accepted terms and
conditions.
28. In this factual matrix, the argument that it is a continuing cause of
action as it pertains to pension, is clearly unacceptable. There is no
question of any direction to the State to restore pension on expiry of
11.5 years or 12 years as prayed for or to refund the amount so
recovered. It is necessarily for the State to take a considered decision
thereon after delving into the complex questions and underlying
parameters which would be involved for assessment of the issues.
Admittedly, matters related to commutation of pension are complex
affairs involving vexed issues traversing diverse field which calls for
application of specialized expertise. It is a settled position that in such
matters the Court would venture only in case of manifest and apparent
arbitrariness. Learned counsel for petitioners were unable to point out
any material on record to indicate that the formula adopted is per se
and ex facie irrational or arbitrary which calls for interference by this
Court.
29. At this stage, we take note of the specific stand of the State as
projected before us that it would be ready to examine the
scheme/period of commutation while taking into consideration
changes, if any, in underlying parameters and that an Expert
Committee would be constituted in this respect which would be
assisted by Recognized Expert Agency or Institution(s) which possess
requisite knowledge and competence in assessing such matters. In this
process the Committee would also invite and consider submission and
representations in the matter from Associations of pensioners in the
State."

192.
17. Learned counsel for the opposite parties have drawn attention of
this Court towards Annexure S.C.A.-1 of the supplementary counter
affidavit-cum-reply to the rejoinder affidavit filed on 24.4.2025 which is a
copy of the office memo dated 4.2.2025 issued by the Additional Chief
Secretary, Department of Finance, State of U.P. By means of aforesaid
office memo dated 4.2.2025 the Additional Chief Secretary, Finance
constituted a committee of 8 specialized / expert persons to look into the
grievance of the retired employees which has been raised in the bunch
of present writ petitions, consisting Secretary, Finance (Services), U.P.
(Chairman); Secretary, Finance & Director General, Institutional Finance
Directorate, U.P. (Member); Special Secretary nominated by the
Principal Secretary, Karmik, U.P. (Member); Any competent officer
nominated by the Principal Secretary, Law, U.P. (Special invited
member); Member, State Finance Commission, U.P. (by name)
(Member); Special Secretary, Finance (General), Anubhag-3, U.P.
(Member Secretary); Director, Pension Directorate, U.P. (Member) and
Director, Treasuries Directorate, U.P. (Member).
18. Further, Para 2 of the aforesaid office memo dated 4.12.2025 says
that the aforesaid committee may take proper help, aid and advise from
any recognized and specialized agency or institution who are having
sufficient knowledge in making calculation relating to the grievance of
the petitioners. Such committee shall invite the representations from the
associations of pensioners and other affected persons to consider the
same. Therefore, the learned counsel for the opposite parties have
stated that the proper committee has already been constituted by the
State Government vide office memo dated 4.2.2025, therefore, the
petitioners may form an association to approach the aforesaid committee
preferring an exhaustive representation ventilating their grievance
enclosing therewith the relevant documents which are necessary for the
disposal of such representation and the committee may dispose of the
grievance of the petitioners by passing speaking and reasoned order by
affording an opportunity of hearing to the office bearers of such
association / pensioners, for that any time schedule may be fixed by this
Court. They have further submitted that the prayer, so prayed by the
petitioners in the bunch of writ petitions may not be allowed at this stage

193.
and liberty may be given to the petitioners to approach this Court if they
feel themselves aggrieved from the orders of the aforesaid committee.
19. In the rejoinder reply the learned counsel for the petitioners have
submitted that the order passed by the Division Bench of this Court in re:
Ashok Kumar Agarwal (supra) is relating to the employees of Punjab
National Bank and their service condition is governed by the Punjab
National Bank (Employees) Pension Regulation, 1995. Chapter VIII
Regulation 41 defines commutation and para 2 thereof clearly provides
that an employee who had commuted the admissible portion of pension
is entitled to have commuted portion of pension restored after the expiry
of the period of 15 years from the date of commutation. However, no
such prescription of law is available with the employees of the State
Government or the other employees who have filed the writ petition in
this bunch. As a matter of fact such provision has been taken from the
impugned government order no. a-3-1587/10-87-558-80 dated
23.7.1987 which has been enclosed as C.A.-2 to the counter affidavit
filed on 13.9.2024. The provision of commutation of pension is governed
by the aforesaid government order, therefore, learned counsel for the
petitioners have stated that in absence of any specific rule or regulation,
the period of commutation of pension should not be treated as 15 years
but it should be treated as 10 years and 11 months or 11 years or at the
most 12 years.
20. Replying the aforesaid contention of learned counsel for the
petitioners the learned counsel for the opposite parties have drawn
attention of this Court towards the judgment and order dated 9.12.1986
passed by the Apex Court in re: 'Common Cause' A Registered
Society and others vs. Union of India reported in AIR 1987 Supreme
Court 210 to submit that after passing the aforesaid judgment of the
Apex Court the Government Order dated 23.7.1987 has been issued in
conformity with the directions of the Apex Court as has been indicated in
para 9 thereof which reads as under :
"9. In dealing with a matter of this nature, it is not appropriate to be guided
by the example of Life Insurance; equally unjust it would be to adopt the
interest basis. On the other hand, the conclusion should be evolved by relating

194.
it to the 'years of purchase' basis. An addition of two years to the period
necessary for the recovery on the basis of years of purchase justifies the
adoption of the 15 years rule. That is more or less the basis which appears to
be equitable. It may be that this would give rise to an addi- tional burden on
the exchequer but it would not be heavy and after all it would bring some
relief to those who have served the cause of the Nation at great sacrifice. We
are, therefore, of the view that no separate period need be fixed for the Armed
Forces personnel and they should also be entitled to restoration of the
commuted portion of the pension on the expiry of 15 years as is conceded in
the case of civil pensioners. And for them too the effective date should be from
1.4.1985.
21. Having heard learned counsel for the parties and having perused
the material available on record it is clear from the records that the Vth
Central Pay Commission and IInd National Judicial Pay Commission
have recommended that it would be reasonable to restore the commuted
portion of pension after 12 years, instead of 15 years and the Punjab
National Bank has got its specific provision for restoration of commuted
portion of pension after 15 years, whereas there is no specific act or rule
or regulation to that effect relating to the employees of the State of U.P.
and such exercise of commutation of pension is being undertaken in the
light of the Government Order dated 13.7.1987 and also subsequent
Government Orders including the Government Order dated 8.12.2008,
therefore, keeping in a view the fact that the Additional Chief Secretary,
Department of Finance, U.P. has constituted a committee vide Office
Memo dated 4.2.2025 for considering and disposing of the grievance of
the petitioners of the bunch of writ petitions who are aggrieved from the
action / inaction on the part of the concerning authorities restoring the
commuted portion of pension after 15 years instead of 10 years 11
months or 11 years or at the most 12 years, I am not entering into the
merits of the issue at this stage, rather I am disposing of the bunch of
writ petitions finally at the admission stage, without interfering the
impugned government orders giving liberty to the petitioners of the
present bunch of writ petitions to approach such committee either
personally or through the office bearers of their Association, if any,
preferring their representation taking all pleas and grounds which are
available to them enclosing therewith the copies of the relevant

195.
documents which are necessary for the disposal of such representation
within a period of one month and if such representation is preferred to
the aforesaid committee, the issue regarding the restoration period
needs to be examined de novo by said committee strictly in accordance
with law by a speaking and reasoned order by affording an opportunity of
hearing to the petitioners with expedition preferably within a period of
three months from the date of production of such representation along
with certified copy of the order of this Court and decision thereof shall be
intimated to the persons who have preferred such representation with
expedition.
22. It is expected from the aforesaid committee that proper help, aid or
advise from expert agency / institution may be taken in terms of para 2 of
the office memo dated 4.2.2025 so that the grivance of the petitioners
could be redressed properly.
23. Till the appropriate decision is taken on the aforesaid
representations of the petitioners or their associations, if any, no
recovery shall be executed from the petitioners of the bunch of this writ
petition.
24. It is needless to say that if the petitioners feel themselves
aggrieved from the order being passed by the aforesaid committee on
their representation, they may approach the competent court of law for
redressal of their grievances, if any.
25. Accordingly, the bunch of all the writ petitions are being disposed
of finally in view of the aforesaid terms.
(Order on WRIT - A No. - 9924 of 2024: Shailendra Kumar Shukla
vs. Union Of India Ministry Of Railways Thru. Secy. Railway Board
And 6 Others)
26. Heard learned counsel for the petitioner Sri Birendra Narain Shukla
and Sri Varun Pandey, learned Central Government Counsel for the
opposite parties.
27. This writ petition has been connected with the bunch of writ petition
bearing Writ-A No. 5617 of 2024 : Dr. Om Prakash Srivastava And

196.
Others vs. State Of U.P. Thru. Addl. Chief Secy. Deptt. Of Finance Lko 3
Others vide order dated 25.10.2024 and an interim order was granted,
which reads as under :
"1. Heard learned counsel for petitioner and learned State Counsel for the
respondents.
2. The petitioner is a retired government servant having superannuated from
different posts from the services of Government of Uttar Pradesh and more
than 10 years period has expired since his retirement. The petitioner is
aggrieved by action on the part of opposite parties by deducting the amount
from monthly pension for a period of 180 months (15 years) whereas the
commutation amount paid at the time of retirement and the interest thereon is
fully recovered in a period of 131 months (10 years and 11 months) or at best
11 years.
3. Learned counsel for petitioner has stated that the manner under which the
amount of recovery has been proposed is patently illegal, arbitrary,
discriminatory and uncalled for. The aforesaid amount is being recovered from
the petitioner from the date of his retirement.
4. Learned counsel for petitioner has submit that the commuted amount
stands satisfied and adjusted within a period of ten years and 11 months and,
therefore, adjusting the aforesaid amount for a period of fifteen years,
amounts to charging interest at more than 12 % per annum and is therefore in
the realm of unjust enrichment.
5. Learned counsel for the petitioner has stated that such grievance has been
noticed by the various High Courts e.g. Kerala High Court and Punjab and
Haryana High Court and granted some interim protection, and as such, the
petitioners filed this writ petition.
6. Attention has been drawn towards Annexure No.7 of the writ petition which
is an interim order passed by this Court on 25.7.2024 relying upon the interim
order granted by High Court of Punjab and Haryana in CWP No.2490 of 2024;
Ram Saroop Jindal versus State of Haryana & Ors. It is submitted that the
aforesaid issue is identical with the issue of the present petitioner.
7. Since the recovery is being executed from the retired employee since his
date of retirement and as per learned counsel for the petitioners such
recovery is patently illegal and arbitrary, therefore, it appears that cause of

197.
action is recurring in nature, hence, a detailed and exhaustive counter affidavit
would be required from the opposite parties.
8. Connect this case with WRIT A No. 5617 of 2024 (Dr. Om Prakash
Srivastava and others Vs. State of U. P. and Others).
9. In the meantime, learned Standing Counsel may file counter affidavit.
10. Until further orders, no further recovery shall be executed from the
petitioner, who has completed 10 years and 11 months from the date of their
superannuation. It is also made clear that in case the writ petition is
dismissed, the benefit granted to the petitioner by means of the interim order
shall be recovered in accordance with law. "
28. Notably, this writ petition is related to the retired employee from the
Railway Department and the provisions of restoration period as 15 years
in the Master Circular No. 61 (2019) RBE No. 211/2019 dated
16.12.2019 issued by the Director Finance (Estt.) Railway Board,
Ministry of Railways, Government of India (Annexure no. 3) has been
assailed.
29. While considering the arguments of learned counsel for the parties
in the aforesaid bunch of writ petitions in para 10 I have noted a fact that
the recommendation of Vth Central Pay Commission along with
commutation of pension whereby considering the various judgments of
the constitutional courts it has been recommended that it would be
reasonable to restore the commuted portion of pension after 12 years,
instead of 15 years. Further, in para 11, I have further noted the
recommendation of the Second National Judicial Pay Commission
having committee of three eminent persons, one is Retired Judge,
Supreme Court of India who is Chairman, one is Former Judge of High
Court, Kerala, Member and Member Secretary of the aforesaid
Commission is District Judge, Delhi whereby the aforesaid pay
commission has recommended, after noticing the judgment of Apex
Court in re: 'Common Cause' A Registered Society and others
(supra) that the aforesaid judgment has lost its relevance in the present
day context and cannot be taken as binding precedence for all times to
come, therefore, restoration period of 12 years suggested by the Vth
Central Pay Commission appears to be more than adequate. It further

198.
recommended that the issue regarding the restoration period needs to
be examined de novo after issue of notice to Government of India
(Department of Pension and Pensioners Welfare).
30. In the bunch of writ petitions where the case of the retired
employee of the State of U.P. and instrumentality thereof is concerned
the State Government has constituted a committee of eight specialist /
expert persons vide Office Memo dated 4.2.2025 but learned counsel for
the Government of India / Railways has not intimated any information to
the effect that such type of committee has been constituted for the
retired employees of the Government of India, or Railway Department,
therefore, I am of the considered opinion that such type of committee
must have been constituted by the competent authority of the Railway
Board / competent authority of the Railway Department, so that the
grievance of the retired employees of the Department could be
redressed properly. I am also of the considered opinion that the
recommendations of Vth Central Pay Commission as well as Second
National Judicial Pay Commission are liable to be considered in a
positive manner. Though the period so prescribed for doing the aforesaid
exercise is 15 years in view of the impugned circular in a same manner
the Government Order dated 23.7.1987 provides for the retired
employees of the State Government as well as instrumentality thereof
but the aforesaid grievance may be examined de novo after issue of
notice to the Government of India, particularly, the Department of
Pension and Pensioners Welfare.
31. Therefore, without entering into the merits of the issue and without
interfering the impugned circular dated 16.12.2019 (Annexure no. 3), I
hereby dispose of this writ petition at the admission stage giving liberty
to the petitioner to prefer a fresh representation to Secretary, Railway
Board, New Delhi i.e. opposite party no. 1 and to Director, Finance
(Estt.) Railway Board, Ministry of Railways, Government of India, Rail
Bhawan, New Delhi, opposite party no. 2 within a period of one month
taking all pleas and grounds which are available to him enclosing
therewith copies of the relevant documents which are necessary for
disposal of the representation of the petitioner and if such representation
is preferred by the petitioner, an expert committee may be formed in a

199.
same manner it has been formed by the State of U.P. and the issue
regarding the restoration period needs to be examined de novo by said
committee strictly in accordance with law by a speaking and reasoned
order by affording an opportunity of hearing to the petitioner with
expedition preferably within a period of three months from the date of
production of such representation along with certified copy of the order
of this Court and decision thereof shall be intimated to the persons who
have preferred such representation with expedition
32. It is also directed that even if no committee is constituted by the
competent authority of Railway Department, the representation of the
petitioner or of the office bearers of the association of retired employees,
as the case may be, shall be decided in terms of order of this Court.
33. Till the appropriate decision is taken on the aforesaid
representation of the petitioner or his association, if any, no recovery
shall be executed from the petitioner.
34. It is needless to say that if the petitioner feels aggrieved from the
order being passed by the aforesaid committee on his representation, he
may approach the competent court of law for redressal of his grievances,
if any.
35. Therefore, this writ petition is being disposed of finally in view of
the aforesaid terms.
.
[ Rajesh Singh Chauhan, J.]
Dated : 28.5.2025
Om.
